Paste is definitely worth the cash if you find yourself doing a lot of copying and pasting.
Paste is unique in the way it does the job. That uniqueness and that clarity coupled to its new iOS companion make Paste a worthwhile app to get.
Paste really is an impressive clipboard manager, and has the nicest user interface, by far.

I use it everyday pasting multiple items from different sources. For me it is indespenable to my writing.
I use Paste everyday - mostly on my MacBook but also on my iPhone and iPad. It is easy to use and very reliable.
I am so thankful for Paste & the time that it saves me at work, with my small business, and for personal projects.
I've been looking for something like this for ages. Well worth the money and once you buy it you'll never go back.

add_item_to_pinboard
Add item to pinboard
Pin a clipboard history item to a pinboard. Idempotent 
 adding the same item twice keeps it pinned once. Returns the updated pinboard summary.
Identifier of the clipboard item (from search results).
Identifier of the destination pinboard (from list_pinboards).
`content` cannot be empty.
Create clipboard item
Create an item from text `content`. Without `list_id` it is added to clipboard history, where it shows up in search. With `list_id` (a private, non-shared pinboard) it is pinned to that pinboard: if the same content is already in history the existing item is pinned (and stays in history), otherwise a new pinned-only item is created that is not added to history. Shared pinboards are not supported. Does not write to the system clipboard. The item's type is auto-detected from the content 
 usually `text`, but content that reads as a 6-digit hex color (e.g. "#FF0000") becomes a `color` and a URL becomes a `link`. Returns the created item summary (id, type, preview, timestamp, app, device); `timestamp` is absent when the item is pinned to a pinboard only (not in history).
Text content for the new item. Stored verbatim.
Optional id of a private (non-shared) pinboard from list_pinboards. When set, the item is pinned to that pinboard instead of going to history; content already in history is pinned rather than duplicated.
`name` cannot be empty.
Create a new pinboard with the given name. Returns the created pinboard summary (id, name, itemCount).
Display name for the new pinboard (whitespace-trimmed).
Preview 
 would delete this item from history and its pinboard, for good. Call again with confirm: true to delete.
Delete saved item
Delete a saved item entirely and irreversibly 
 from clipboard history and the pinboard it's pinned to, removing the underlying record for good. Destructive, two-step: call without `confirm` to preview, then with `confirm: true` to delete. To only unpin an item while keeping it, use remove_item_from_pinboard.
Identifier of the item to delete (from search results).
Must be true to actually delete. Omit or false to preview only.
Preview 
 would delete pinboard "
 items). Call again with confirm: true to delete. 
Pinned items stay in clipboard history.
Deleted pinboard "
Delete a pinboard. Destructive, two-step: call without `confirm` to preview (nothing changes), then with `confirm: true` to delete. Pinned items stay in clipboard history.
Identifier of the pinboard to delete (from list_pinboards).
List the user's pinboards. Returns flat summaries (id, name, itemCount). Use the returned id with add_item_to_pinboard / remove_item_from_pinboard / rename_pinboard.

Read the full content of a saved item by id (returned by search). Text comes back verbatim, cut at 
 characters if it is longer (structured output then carries `truncated` and `totalLength`); links return their URL; colors return a `#RRGGBB` hex string; files return their paths one per line; images return the image itself (downscaled and re-encoded) for vision-capable clients.

remove_item_from_pinboard
Remove item from pinboard
Unpin an item from a pinboard. The item stays in clipboard history if it was there; only the pinning is removed. Returns the updated pinboard summary.
Identifier of the clipboard item to unpin.
Identifier of the pinboard to unpin the item from.
Rename an existing pinboard. Returns the updated pinboard summary.
Identifier of the pinboard to rename (from list_pinboards).
New display name (whitespace-trimmed).
`date_from` must be before `date_to`.
` must be an ISO-8601 date or date-time: 
Search saved items
Search the user's saved items 
 clipboard history and pinboards both 
 with optional filters. With no arguments, returns the most recent items. `query` is free text matched against titles and content. Filters (combinable): `type` (see allowed values); `app` / `device` 
 match the `app` / `device` field shown on returned items; `pinboard_id` 
 a pinboard id from `list_pinboards`; `title` 
 a title substring; `date_from` / `date_to` 
 ISO-8601 bounds for a half-open timestamp range (either may be omitted for an open end). Returns flat summaries (id, type, preview, timestamp, app, device); `timestamp` is when the item entered clipboard history (ISO-8601), absent for pinned-only items; call read_item for full content. Results are ordered newest first. A search resolves at most 100 matches, the 100 most recent 
 narrow the query rather than raising `limit` to reach older items.
Free text matched against item titles and content.
Item type filter.
Source application filter 
 matches the `app` field shown on returned items.
Device filter 
 matches the `device` field shown on returned items.
Title substring filter.
Restrict to items pinned to this pinboard (id from list_pinboards).
ISO-8601 start (inclusive) of the timestamp range, e.g. 2026-05-01T00:00:00Z. A date-only value (e.g. 2026-05-01) starts at the beginning of that day.
ISO-8601 end of the timestamp range. A full date-time is exclusive; a date-only value (e.g. 2026-05-01) covers that whole day.
Maximum number of items to return. Defaults to 20, capped at 100.
Pass at least one of `title` or `content` to update.
Cannot edit the content of an image or file item. You can still change its title.
Update saved item
Update a saved item's `title`, its `content`, or both (at least one is required). Passing `title` sets the item's title; an empty string clears it. Passing `content` replaces the body and re-runs type auto-detection (text/color/link), exactly like create_item. Content edits are rejected for image and file items (their title can still be changed). Returns the updated item summary.
Identifier of the item to update (from search results).
New title. Pass an empty string to clear the title.
New text content. Replaces the body and re-detects the type.
